{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_4fccd480539c451ebe3956c1","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# bead-forge Implementation Plan\n2\t\n3\t> **User-facing documentation:** [`../README.md`](../README.md) — overview, command reference, NEEDLE integration guide, and hero image.\n4\t\n5\t## Overview\n6\t\n7\t**bead-forge** (`bf`) is a Rust binary that replaces `br` (beads_rust). SQLite is the live database — all reads and writes go through it. `issues.jsonl` is a git-synced artifact exported on demand: one entry per bead, committed to the repo for backup, sharing, and recovery. Concurrent claiming is handled by SQLite's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction — no server, no daemon, no flock required.\n8\t\n9\t## Core Principles\n10\t\n11\t1. **SQLite is the live store** — all mutations are SQL writes; reads are SQL queries. Fast, concurrent-read, serialized-write via WAL mode.\n12\t2. **JSONL is the git artifact** — exported on `bf sync`, committed to git, one entry per bead. Used for backup, cross-machine sharing, and recovery. Never written during normal operation.\n13\t3. **Drop-in CLI** — all `br` commands work under `bf` with the same flags and output formats\n14\t4. **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for atomic claim** — the read-score-update sequence runs inside a single SQLite write transaction, eliminating the race condition `br` has without requiring flock or a daemon\n15\t\n16\t---\n17\t\n18\t## Phase 1: Core Library — Data Model & JSONL Import/Export\n19\t\n20\t### 1.1 Data Model (`src/model.rs`)\n21\t\n22\tPort the exact `Issue`, `Status`, `Priority`, `IssueType`, `Dependency`, `DependencyType`, `Comment`, and `Event` structs from beads_rust with identical Serde attributes. This is the wire format — every field, every `skip_serializing_if`, every rename must match.\n23\t\n24\tKey structs and their JSONL serialization:\n25\t\n26\t```\n27\tStatus:       open | in_progress | blocked | deferred | draft | closed | tombstone | pinned | Custom(String)\n28\tPriority:     transparent i32 (0=Critical, 4=Backlog)\n29\tIssueType:    task | bug | feature | epic | chore | docs | question | Custom(String)\n30\tDependency:   { issue_id, depends_on_id, type (renamed from dep_type), created_at, created_by?, metadata?, thread_id? }\n31\tComment:      { id: i64, issue_id, author, text (renamed from body), created_at }\n32\tEvent:        { id: i64, issue_id, event_type, actor, old_value?, new_value?, comment?, created_at }\n33\t```\n34\t\n35\tIssue fields (alphabetical by JSON key):\n36\t```\n37\tid, title, description?, design?, acceptance_criteria?, notes?,\n38\tstatus, priority, issue_type, assignee?, owner?, estimated_minutes?,\n39\tcreated_at, created_by?, updated_at, closed_at?, close_reason?,\n40\tclosed_by_session?, due_at?, defer_until?, external_ref?,\n41\tsource_system?, source_repo, deleted_at?, deleted_by?, delete_reason?,\n42\toriginal_type?, compaction_level (default 0), compacted_at?,\n43\tcompacted_at_commit?, original_size?, sender?, ephemeral (default false),\n44\tpinned (default false), is_template (default false),\n45\tlabels: Vec<String>, dependencies: Vec<Dependency>, comments: Vec<Comment>\n46\t```\n47\t\n48\t### 1.2 JSONL Import (`src/jsonl.rs`)\n49\t\n50\tJSONL is read only during `bf sync --import` (pulling state from git) and `bf doctor --repair` (rebuilding SQLite from the artifact). Never read during normal CLI operation.\n51\t\n52\t```rust\n53\tfn import_jsonl(jsonl_path: &Path, storage: &Storage) -> Result<ImportResult> {\n54\t    let file = File::open(jsonl_path)?;\n55\t    let reader = BufReader::new(file);\n56\t    storage.with_write_transaction(|tx| {\n57\t        for line in reader.lines() {\n58\t            let issue: Issue = serde_json::from_str(&line?)?;\n59\t            tx.upsert_issue(&issue)?;\n60\t        }\n61\t        Ok(())\n62\t    })\n63\t}\n64\t```\n65\t\n66\tFunctions:\n67\t- `import_jsonl(path, storage)` — bulk upsert from JSONL into SQLite\n68\t- `stream_issues(path) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<Issue>>` — lazy line iterator (used by import and doctor)\n69\t\n70\t### 1.3 JSONL Export (`src/jsonl.rs`)\n71\t\n72\tJSONL is written only during `bf sync --flush` (preparing the git artifact). Exports current bead state from SQLite, one entry per bead, sorted by ID for stable diffs. Atomic temp+rename.\n73\t\n74\t```rust\n75\tfn export_jsonl(jsonl_path: &Path, storage: &Storage) -> Result<ExportResult> {\n76\t    let issues = storage.list_all_issues()?;  // sorted by ID\n77\t    let temp_path = jsonl_path.with_extension(\"jsonl.tmp\");\n78\t    let mut writer = BufWriter::new(File::create_new(&temp_path)?);\n79\t    for issue in &issues {\n80\t        serde_json::to_writer(&mut writer, issue)?;\n81\t        writer.write_all(b\"\\n\")?;\n82\t    }\n83\t    writer.flush()?;\n84\t    drop(writer);\n85\t    fs::rename(&temp_path, jsonl_path)?;\n86\t    Ok(ExportResult { count: issues.len() })\n87\t}\n88\t```\n89\t\n90\tFunctions:\n91\t- `export_jsonl(path, storage)` — dump all beads from SQLite to JSONL (atomic)\n92\t- `export_jsonl_dirty(path, storage)` — export only dirty beads (incremental, faster for large workspaces)\n93\t\n94\t### 1.4 Config (`src/config.rs`)\n95\t\n96\tParse `.beads/config.yaml` with the same fields br uses:\n97\t\n98\t```yaml\n99\tissue_prefixes: [bd, fg]\n100\tdefault_priority: 2\n101\tdefault_type: task\n102\tscoring:\n103\t  priority_weight: 0.4\n104\t  blockers_weight: 0.3\n105\t  age_weight: 0.2\n106\t  labels_weight: 0.1\n107\t  max_age_hours: 20\n108\t  max_blockers: 3\n109\t# bead-forge additions (additive, br ignores these)\n110\tclaim_ttl_minutes: 30\n111\t```\n112\t\n113\tParse `.beads/metadata.json`:\n114\t```json\n115\t{ \"database\": \"beads.db\", \"jsonl_export\": \"issues.jsonl\" }\n116\t```\n117\t\n118\t### 1.5 ID Generation (`src/id.rs`)\n119\t\n120\tPort br's ID scheme: `<prefix>-<hash>` where hash is base36 lowercase. Use the birthday-problem adaptive length (3-8 chars). Read `issue_prefixes` from config (first entry is the default prefix).\n121\t\n122\t```rust\n123\tfn generate_id(prefix: &str, existing_count: usize) -> String {\n124\t    let len = optimal_hash_length(existing_count);\n125\t    let random_bytes = rand::random::<[u8; 16]>();\n126\t    let hash = sha256(&random_bytes);\n127\t    base36_encode(&hash[..len])\n128\t    format!(\"{}-{}\", prefix, hash)\n129\t}\n130\t```\n131\t\n132\t---\n133\t\n134\t## Phase 2: SQLite Primary Store\n135\t\n136\t### 2.1 Schema Application (`src/storage/schema.rs`)\n137\t\n138\tApply the exact same `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` DDL from br's `SCHEMA_SQL`. Tables:\n139\t\n140\t| Table | Purpose |\n141\t|-------|---------|\n142\t| `issues` | All 35 columns, same types, defaults, CHECK constraints |\n143\t| `dependencies` | (issue_id, depends_on_id) PK, type, metadata, thread_id |\n144\t| `labels` | (issue_id, label) PK |\n145\t| `comments` | AUTOINCREMENT id, issue_id, author, text, created_at |\n146\t| `events` | AUTOINCREMENT id, issue_id, event_type, actor, old/new/comment |\n147\t| `config` | key-value |\n148\t| `metadata` | key-value |\n149\t| `dirty_issues` | Track which issues need JSONL export |\n150\t| `export_hashes` | Incremental export tracking |\n151\t| `blocked_issues_cache` | Materialized view of blocked issues |\n152\t| `child_counters` | Hierarchical IDs (bd-abc.1, bd-abc.2) |\n153\t| `recovery_sessions` | Anomaly/recovery audit trail |\n154\t| `anomaly_audit` | Recovery event tracking |\n155\t| `bead_annotations` | bf-only: arbitrary key-value metadata per bead; separate table so br's `rebuild_issues_table()` never touches it |\n156\t\n157\tAll indexes from br must be created identically, including the critical `idx_issues_ready` composite partial index.\n158\t\n159\t### 2.2 Storage Engine (`src/storage/sqlite.rs`)\n160\t\n161\tUse `rusqlite` (not FrankenSQLite) bundled with system SQLite. Apply WAL mode, foreign keys, busy_timeout=30s, cache_size=-8000, synchronous=NORMAL.\n162\t\n163\tAll mutations go through here. JSONL is never touched during normal operation.\n164\t\n165\tFunctions:\n166\t- `open(path: &Path) -> Result<Storage>` — open/create DB, apply schema\n167\t- `get_issue(id: &str) -> Result<Option<Issue>>` — point lookup by ID\n168\t- `list_issues(filter: IssueFilter) -> Result<Vec<Issue>>` — filtered query with WHERE clauses\n169\t- `list_all_issues() -> Result<Vec<Issue>>` — full table scan sorted by ID (for JSONL export)\n170\t- `create_issue(issue: &Issue) -> Result<()>` — INSERT\n171\t- `update_issue(id: &str, changes: IssueChanges) -> Result<()>` — UPDATE + mark dirty\n172\t- `close_issue(id: &str, reason: &str) -> Result<()>` — status transition + mark dirty\n173\t- `with_write_transaction(f) -> Result<T>` — execute closure in a write transaction\n174\t- `with_immediate_transaction(f) -> Result<T>` — `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` with retry (see §2.5) — acquires write lock before any reads (used by claim)\n175\t- `rebuild_blocked_cache() -> Result<()>` — recalculate blocked issues materialized view\n176\t\n177\t### 2.3 Sync Protocol (`src/sync.rs`)\n178\t\n179\tPort br's sync semantics:\n180\t\n181\t**Flush (SQLite → JSONL, for git)**:\n182\t1. Call `export_jsonl_dirty` — query dirty bead IDs, export full current state of each from SQLite\n183\t2. Atomic temp+rename over `issues.jsonl`\n184\t3. Clear `dirty_issues`, update `export_hashes`\n185\t\n186\tFor `bf sync` (full flush): call `export_jsonl` — dump all beads sorted by ID, unconditionally.\n187\t\n188\t**Import (JSONL → SQLite, from git pull)**:\n189\t1. Stream `issues.jsonl` line by line\n190\t2. Compare each with SQLite state using `content_hash`\n191\t3. INSERT new, UPDATE changed, SKIP unchanged\n192\t4. Handle collisions (same ID, different content) with deterministic resolution\n193\t\n194\t### 2.5 Concurrent Write Safety (`src/storage/sqlite.rs`)\n195\t\n196\tSQLite WAL mode serializes concurrent writers through a single write lock — they queue, not corrupt. `SQLITE_BUSY` is returned when a writer cannot acquire the lock within `busy_timeout`. With claim latency of ~1-2ms and a 20-worker fleet, the entire queue clears in ~20-40ms, so `SQLITE_BUSY` should never occur in normal operation. However, a retry wrapper is the cheap safety net that makes this a guarantee rather than an assumption:\n197\t\n198\t```rust\n199\tconst MAX_RETRIES: u32 = 5;\n200\tconst RETRY_BASE_MS: u64 = 50;\n201\t\n202\tfn with_immediate_transaction<T>(\n203\t    conn: &Connection,\n204\t    f: impl Fn(&Transaction) -> Result<T>,\n205\t) -> Result<T> {\n206\t    let mut attempt = 0;\n207\t    loop {\n208\t        match conn.execute(\"BEGIN IMMEDIATE\", []) {\n209\t            Ok(_) => {\n210\t                let tx = conn.unchecked_transaction()?;\n211\t                return match f(&tx) {\n212\t                    Ok(val) => { tx.commit()?; Ok(val) }\n213\t                    Err(e) => { let _ = tx.rollback(); Err(e) }\n214\t                };\n215\t            }\n216\t            Err(e) if is_busy_error(&e) && attempt < MAX_RETRIES => {\n217\t                attempt += 1;\n218\t                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(RETRY_BASE_MS * attempt as u64));\n219\t                continue;\n220\t            }\n221\t            Err(e) => return Err(e.into()),\n222\t        }\n223\t    }\n224\t}\n225\t\n226\tfn is_busy_error(e: &rusqlite::Error) -> bool {\n227\t    matches!(\n228\t        e,\n229\t        rusqlite::Error::SqliteFailure(\n230\t            rusqlite::ffi::Error { code: rusqlite::ErrorCode::DatabaseBusy, .. },\n231\t            _\n232\t        )\n233\t    )\n234\t}\n235\t```\n236\t\n237\t**Why this is sufficient and not a workaround**: `SQLITE_BUSY` with WAL mode is not data corruption — it is a clean \"lock unavailable, try again\" signal. The database is fully consistent when this occurs. The retry converts a transient contention spike (e.g., 20 workers all starting within the same millisecond) into a short wait rather than a user-visible error.\n238\t\n239\t**All write operations** (`claim`, `create`, `update`, `close`, `batch`) use `with_immediate_transaction`. Read-only operations (`list`, `show`, `ready`) use a plain `BEGIN DEFERRED` transaction and are never affected by write contention.\n240\t\n241\t### 2.7 Doctor/Repair (`src/doctor.rs`)\n242\t\n243\t- `doctor --repair`: Delete `beads.db`, reimport from JSONL\n244\t- `doctor --check`: Validate JSONL line integrity, check SQLite <-> JSONL consistency\n245\t- `doctor --repair --flush-first`: Flush unflushed beads to JSONL before repairing (protects against data loss)\n246\t- `doctor --repair --force`: Force repair even with unflushed beads (WARNING: unflushed beads are lost)\n247\t- **Flush-before-repair rule**: Always run `bf sync --flush-only` before `bf doctor --repair` to protect against losing unflushed beads\n248\t- Recovery from corruption: JSONL export is the authoritative checkpoint for recovery — `beads.db` can be rebuilt from it at any time\n249\t\n250\t**CRITICAL DATA AUTHORITY NOTE**: bead-forge inverts the authority model from upstream beads/br:\n251\t\n252\t| Tool | Source of truth | Checkpoint role |\n253\t|------|----------------|-----------------|\n254\t| beads / br | JSONL (`issues.jsonl`) | SQLite is read-cache |\n255\t| bead-forge | SQLite (`beads.db`) | JSONL is git-tracked checkpoint |\n256\t\n257\tThis inversion is necessary for atomic multi-worker operations. **Beads created or modified since the last `bf sync --flush-only` exist only in SQLite**. Running `bf doctor --repair` without flushing first destroys these unflushed beads.\n258\t\n259\t**Historical context**: On 2026-06-10, seven independent agents across seven workspaces (ARMOR, NEEDLE, AgentScribe, kalshi-weather, jedarden.com, vibe-coding-discovery, face/pose/sun repos) each lost their entire first batch of freshly created beads by running `doctor --repair` after bulk creates. Four db-only beads in ARMOR (bf-4rm7/5zxa/tojg/tr44) were permanently lost. This fix implements the flush-before-repair protection.\n260\t\n261\t---\n262\t\n263\t## Phase 3: CLI Commands\n264\t\n265\t### 3.1 Command Structure (`src/cli/`)\n266\t\n267\tUse `clap` with derive macros. Binary name: `bf`. Subcommands mirror br exactly.\n268\t\n269\t```\n270\tbf create    --title \"...\" --type <type> --priority <N> [--description \"...\"] [--label ...]\n271\tbf list      [--status <s>] [--type <t>] [--assignee <a>] [--limit N] [--format json|text|toon]\n272\tbf show      <id>\n273\tbf update    <id> [--status <s>] [--assignee <a>] [--priority <N>] [--title \"...\"] ...\n274\tbf close     <id> [--reason \"...\"]\n275\tbf reopen    <id>\n276\tbf delete    <id>\n277\tbf ready         [--limit N]                     # unblocked, open, sorted by priority\n278\tbf claim         --assignee <id>                 # atomic claim (see Phase 4)\n279\t                 [--model <model>]               # e.g. claude-sonnet-4-6\n280\t                 [--harness <name>]              # e.g. needle\n281\t                 [--harness-version <ver>]       # e.g. 0.5.2\n282\t                 [--workspace <path>] [--any]\n283\tbf critical-path <epic-id>                       # show critical path + float for each bead\n284\tbf dep           add-blocker <parent> <child>\n285\tbf dep           remove-blocker <parent> <child>\n286\tbf dep           tree <id>\n287\tbf label         <id> add <label>\n288\tbf label         <id> remove <label>\n289\tbf comment       <id> [--author <a>] [--text \"...\"]\n290\tbf sync          --flush-only\n291\tbf sync          --import-only\n292\tbf doctor        [--repair]\n293\tbf migrate       [<workspace>]                  # migrate br workspace to bf (see Phase 4C)\n294\t                 [--from-jsonl]                 # rebuild SQLite from JSONL + git history\n295\t                 [--seed-velocity]              # populate velocity_stats from events history\n296\t                 [--dry-run]\n297\tbf init          [--prefix <p>]\n298\tbf stats\n299\tbf count         [--status <s>]\n300\tbf search        <query>\n301\tbf schema\n302\tbf config        get <key>\n303\tbf config        set <key> <value>\n304\t```\n305\t\n306\t### 3.2 Output Formats\n307\t\n308\tThree formats, matching br exactly:\n309\t- **text**: Human-readable table (default for terminal)\n310\t- **json**: One JSON object per line (for piping)\n311\t- **toon**: Token-optimized output for LLM context windows\n312\t\n313\t### 3.3 Execution Model\n314\t\n315\tEach command:\n316\t1. Locate `.beads/` directory (walk up from CWD)\n317\t2. Load config + metadata\n318\t3. Open SQLite (`beads.db`), apply schema if new\n319\t4. Execute: mutations are SQL writes; reads are SQL queries\n320\t5. `bf sync` is the only command that touches `issues.jsonl` — flush exports it, import reads it\n321\t\n322\t---\n323\t\n324\t## Phase 4: Concurrent Claiming\n325\t\n326\t### 4.1 Architecture: SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`\n327\t\n328\tThe thundering herd problem — N workers racing to claim the same bead — is solved by running the entire read-score-update sequence inside a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. SQLite's write lock is acquired before any reads, so no two workers can read the same candidate state. No flock, no server, no daemon.\n329\t\n330\t```\n331\tWorker 1: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT candidates → score → UPDATE winner → COMMIT\n332\tWorker 2: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → (blocked at write lock until Worker 1 COMMITs) → SELECT → score → UPDATE → COMMIT\n333\tWorker 3: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → (blocked) → ...\n334\t...\n335\tWorker 20: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → (blocked) → SELECT → NONE (queue empty) → COMMIT (no-op)\n336\t```\n337\t\n338\tEach claim is a single CLI invocation. The critical section (lock held) is: SELECT scoring fields → score in memory → UPDATE winner + stale resets → COMMIT. Lock held for ~0.5-2ms.\n339\t\n340\t**Throughput**: SQLite handles claims purely in memory after the initial page cache warm-up:\n341\t\n342\t| Workspace | Active beads | Claim latency | Throughput |\n343\t|-----------|-------------|---------------|-----------|\n344\t| Typical (FORGE) | 248 | ~0.5ms | ~2000/sec |\n345\t| Medium (NEEDLE) | 518 | ~1ms | ~1000/sec |\n346\t| Large | 2000 | ~2ms | ~500/sec |\n347\t\n348\tWith 20 workers claiming simultaneously, the last worker waits ~20-40ms (19 queued transactions × ~1-2ms each). The fleet needs ~1-2 claims/sec at steady state — SQLite is overkill by 3 orders of magnitude.\n349\t\n350\t### 4.2 Atomic Claim (`src/claim.rs`)\n351\t\n352\tThe entire claim sequence runs inside a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. SQLite acquires the write lock before any reads, so no two workers can observe the same candidate state.\n353\t\n354\t```rust\n355\tfn claim(storage: &Storage, worker: &str, claim_ttl: Duration) -> Result<Option<Issue>> {\n356\t    storage.with_immediate_transaction(|tx| {\n357\t        let now = Utc::now();\n358\t\n359\t        // 1. Reclaim stale in_progress beads\n360\t        tx.execute(\n361\t            \"UPDATE issues SET status='open', assignee=NULL, updated_at=?1\n362\t             WHERE status='in_progress' AND updated_at < ?2\",\n363\t            params![now, now - claim_ttl],\n364\t        )?;\n365\t\n366\t        // 2. Load scoring fields for open, unblocked candidates\n367\t        //    (blocked_issues_cache is a materialized view maintained on dep changes)\n368\t        let candidates: Vec<ScoreEntry> = tx.query_map(\n369\t            \"SELECT id, priority, updated_at,\n370\t                    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM dependencies d\n371\t                     JOIN issues b ON b.id = d.depends_on_id\n372\t                     WHERE d.issue_id IN (\n373\t                         SELECT issue_id FROM dependencies\n374\t                         WHERE depends_on_id = issues.id AND type='blocks'\n375\t                     ) AND b.status = 'open') AS downstream_impact\n376\t             FROM issues\n377\t             WHERE status = 'open'\n378\t               AND id NOT IN (SELECT issue_id FROM blocked_issues_cache)\n379\t             ORDER BY downstream_impact DESC, priority ASC, created_at ASC\",\n380\t            [],\n381\t            |row| ScoreEntry::from_row(row),\n382\t        )?;\n383\t\n384\t        if candidates.is_empty() {\n385\t            return Ok(None);\n386\t        }\n387\t\n388\t        // 3. Claim the top candidate\n389\t        let winner = &candidates[0];\n390\t        tx.execute(\n391\t            \"UPDATE issues SET status='in_progress', assignee=?1, updated_at=?2\n392\t             WHERE id=?3\",\n393\t            params![worker, now, winner.id],\n394\t        )?;\n395\t        tx.execute(\n396\t            \"INSERT INTO events (issue_id, event_type, actor, old_value, new_value, created_at)\n397\t             VALUES (?1, 'claimed', ?2, 'open', 'in_progress', ?3)\",\n398\t            params![winner.id, worker, now],\n399\t        )?;\n400\t        tx.mark_dirty(&winner.id)?;\n401\t\n402\t        Ok(Some(tx.get_issue(&winner.id)?))\n403\t    })\n404\t}\n405\t```\n406\t\n407\t**Key properties**:\n408\t- **Correctness**: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` holds the SQLite write lock for the entire read-score-update sequence. No two workers can observe the same candidate state.\n409\t- **Crash safety**: If the process crashes mid-transaction, SQLite rolls back automatically. No partial state is committed.\n410\t- **Stale reclamation is a side effect**: The first statement in every claim resets expired `in_progress` beads before scoring. No separate process.\n411\t- **Impact-weighted scoring**: The query orders by `downstream_impact` (beads that would unblock the most work) then priority, then FIFO.\n412\t- **No flock, no file I/O**: The entire claim is in-memory SQL execution after page cache warm-up. `issues.jsonl` is not touched.\n413\t\n414\t### 4.3 Multi-Workspace Claiming (`src/claim.rs`)\n415\t\n416\tFor `bf claim --any` (claim from any workspace), open each workspace's SQLite, run the scoring query on each, pick the global winner, claim it:\n417\t\n418\t```rust\n419\tfn claim_any(workspace_paths: &[PathBuf], worker: &str, claim_ttl: Duration) -> Result<Option<Issue>> {\n420\t    // Score across all workspaces — each Storage::open is cheap (WAL, page cache)\n421\t    let mut best: Option<(Score, PathBuf)> = None;\n422\t    for path in workspace_paths {\n423\t        let storage = Storage::open(&path.join(\".beads/beads.db\"))?;\n424\t        if let Some(score) = storage.top_candidate_score(claim_ttl)? {\n425\t            if best.as_ref().map(|(b, _)| score > *b).unwrap_or(true) {\n426\t                best = Some((score, path.clone()));\n427\t            }\n428\t        }\n429\t    }\n430\t\n431\t    match best {\n432\t        None => Ok(None),\n433\t        Some((_, path)) => {\n434\t            let storage = Storage::open(&path.join(\".beads/beads.db\"))?;\n435\t            claim(&storage, worker, claim_ttl)\n436\t        }\n437\t    }\n438\t}\n439\t```\n440\t\n441\tNo cross-workspace locking needed — each `claim()` call uses `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on its own SQLite file. Two workers racing on the same workspace are serialized by SQLite; two workers racing on different workspaces proceed in parallel.\n442\t\n443\t### 4.4 Stale Claim Detection\n444\t\n445\tNo background process needed. Stale detection runs as part of every `bf claim` invocation:\n446\t\n447\t1. Inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`: `UPDATE issues SET status='open' WHERE status='in_progress' AND updated_at < now - claim_ttl`\n448\t2. This runs before candidate scoring — stale beads become eligible again in the same transaction\n449\t3. Single SQL statement, no extra I/O\n450\t\n451\t**Claim TTL**: Default 30 minutes, configurable in `.beads/config.yaml`:\n452\t\n453\t```yaml\n454\tclaim_ttl_minutes: 30\n455\t```\n456\t\n457\t**TTL tuning**: With 20 workers and tasks taking 5-15 minutes, 30 minutes is conservative. A worker that's actively working but slow will have its bead remain `in_progress` — stale reclamation only triggers if the worker hasn't updated the bead in 30 minutes. Since the worker closes the bead when done (writing a new entry), active workers never get reclaimed.\n458\t\n459\t**Manual stale scan**: For workspaces with no active claiming (no fleet running), `bf doctor --reclaim-stale` performs the same scan without claiming a bead. Can be run via cron for long-running workspaces with occasional stuck claims.\n460\t\n461\t### 4.5 CLI Usage\n462\t\n463\t```bash\n464\t# Claim from current workspace\n465\tbf claim --assignee worker-7 --json\n466\t\n467\t# Claim with worker metadata (feeds velocity-aware scoring — see §4B.6)\n468\tbf claim --assignee worker-7 \\\n469\t         --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \\\n470\t         --harness needle \\\n471\t         --harness-version 0.5.2 \\\n472\t         --json\n473\t\n474\t# Claim from a specific workspace\n475\tbf claim --workspace containers/zai-proxy/ --assignee worker-3 --json\n476\t\n477\t# Claim highest priority across all workspaces\n478\tbf claim --any --assignee worker-12 --json\n479\t\n480\t# Prefer a workspace, fall back to any\n481\tbf claim --workspace containers/zai-proxy/ --fallback any --assignee worker-5 --json\n482\t```\n483\t\n484\tAll commands work identically in all modes — no server required, no daemon running. Each `bf claim` is a self-contained CLI invocation: lock, read, claim, write, unlock, exit.\n485\t\n486\tWorker metadata (`--model`, `--harness`, `--harness-version`) is stored in the claim event row and the `worker_sessions` table. It drives velocity-aware scoring (§4B.6) and appears in `bf log` output so you can see exactly which model/harness combination claimed each bead.\n487\t\n488\t### 4.6 Why No Server\n489\t\n490\tThe plan originally included a coordination server with `Arc<Mutex<ServerState>>`, in-memory priority queues, heartbeats, and a Unix socket protocol. This was removed because:\n491\t\n492\t1. **SQLite is sufficient**: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` provides serialized write access. Claim latency is ~0.5-2ms — 4-16× faster than the previous flock+streaming-rewrite approach.\n493\t2. **Simpler operations**: No flock infrastructure, no streaming rewrite, no temp files during normal operation. All mutation logic is SQL.\n494\t3. **Crash recovery is automatic**: SQLite rolls back incomplete transactions on next open. No partial state, no temp file cleanup.\n495\t4. **Real-world scale validates it**: A 2,000-bead workspace takes ~2ms per claim. A 20-worker fleet needs ~1-2 claims/sec. SQLite delivers ~500 claims/sec at that size.\n496\t5. **Server is a future optimization**: A persistent in-memory server would reduce per-claim latency from ~2ms to ~0.1ms by eliminating SQLite file I/O entirely. Premature for current scale — can be added transparently later.\n497\t\n498\t### 4.7 Comparison to br's Race Condition\n499\t\n500\t| Scenario | br (beads_rust) | bf (bead-forge) |\n501\t|----------|-----------------|-----------------|\n502\t| 20 workers claim simultaneously | 20 read same JSONL, all see same \"next\" bead, all write `in_progress` — last writer wins, 19 workers have phantom claims | 20 workers serialized by SQLite write lock — each BEGIN IMMEDIATE sees post-commit state, each gets a distinct bead |\n503\t| Worker crashes mid-claim | N/A (no lock) | SQLite rolls back the incomplete transaction automatically on next open |\n504\t| Stale claim (worker died) | Bead stays `in_progress` forever | Next claim invocation reclaims beads older than TTL |\n505\t| Cross-workspace claiming | Not supported | `--any` locks all JSONL files in sorted order |\n506\t\n507\t---\n508\t\n509\t## Phase 4B: Extended Features\n510\t\n511\t### 4B.1 JSONL Rotation (`src/rotate.rs`)\n512\t\n513\tKeep `issues.jsonl` lean for fast claiming. With one entry per bead, the file grows only when new beads are created — but over a long project lifetime the accumulation of closed beads still grows the file. Rotation evicts closed beads into numbered archive files, keeping the active scan set small.\n514\t\n515\t```\n516\t.beads/\n517\t├── issues.jsonl          # active: open, in_progress, blocked, deferred + recently closed\n518\t├── issues.jsonl.1        # archive: closed beads rotated out most recently\n519\t├── issues.jsonl.2        # archive: older closed beads\n520\t└── issues.jsonl.3        # archive: oldest\n521\t```\n522\t\n523\t**Rotation rules**:\n524\t- `bf rotate` moves closed beads older than `rotate_age_days` (default 30) from `issues.jsonl` into `issues.jsonl.1` via streaming rewrite (active file) + streaming append (archive file)\n525\t- When `issues.jsonl.1` exceeds `rotate_max_size` (default 5MB), it becomes `issues.jsonl.2`, `.2` → `.3`, etc.\n526\t- Maximum archive files: `rotate_max_archives` (default 5). Oldest archive is deleted.\n527\t\n528\t**Claim only reads the active file**. This keeps claim latency proportional to the number of active (non-closed) beads rather than total workspace history.\n529\t\n530\t**Reading archived beads**: `bf show <id>` searches the active file first, then archives. `bf list --all` includes archives. `bf log <id>` searches all files for the full history.\n531\t\n532\t**git integration**: `.beads/issues.jsonl` is committed to git. Archive files (`.jsonl.1`, `.2`, ...) are also committed — they're part of the project history. The `.beads/` directory is the bead store and should be version-controlled alongside the code.\n533\t\n534\t**Secret protection**: Before any write, bf scans bead fields for secret patterns (AWS keys, private keys, API tokens, passwords) using a built-in regex set similar to git-secrets. If a match is found:\n535\t- `bf create` / `bf update` prints a warning and refuses the write\n536\t- `bf commit-check` (git pre-commit hook) scans staged changes to `.beads/` for secrets\n537\t- Patterns are configurable in `.beads/config.yaml`:\n538\t\n539\t```yaml\n540\tsecret_protection:\n541\t  enabled: true\n542\t  # Additional patterns (matched against all string fields)\n543\t  deny_patterns:\n544\t    - \"-----BEGIN (RSA |EC |DSA )?PRIVATE KEY-----\"\n545\t    - \"AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}\"          # AWS access key\n546\t    - \"sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{32,}\"       # OpenAI/Anthropic API key\n547\t  # Fields to exclude from scanning (e.g., known test values)\n548\t  allowlist_fields: []\n549\t```\n550\t\n551\tConfig:\n552\t\n553\t```yaml\n554\trotate_age_days: 30\n555\trotate_max_size_mb: 5\n556\trotate_max_archives: 5\n557\t```\n558\t\n559\t### 4B.2 Batch Operations (`src/batch.rs`)\n560\t\n561\tExecute multiple operations atomically under a single SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. Replaces sequences of N separate CLI invocations with one.\n562\t\n563\t```bash\n564\t# From a file\n565\tbf batch --file operations.json\n566\t\n567\t# From stdin (one operation per line)\n568\techo 'create --title \"child 1\" --type task\n569\tcreate --title \"child 2\" --type task\n570\tdep add-blocker fg-a3f8.1 fg-a3f8\n571\tdep add-blocker fg-a3f8.2 fg-a3f8\n572\tclose fg-a3f8 --reason \"Split into children\"' | bf batch\n573\t\n574\t# Programmatic JSON input\n575\t# NEEDLE mitosis: use @0, @1 to reference created beads by position\n576\tbf batch --json '[\n577\t  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"child 1\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n578\t  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"child 2\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n579\t  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@0\", \"child\": \"fg-a3f8\"},\n580\t  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@1\", \"child\": \"fg-a3f8\"},\n581\t  {\"op\": \"close\", \"id\": \"fg-a3f8\", \"reason\": \"Split into children\"}\n582\t]'\n583\t```\n584\t\n585\t**Placeholder references**: `@0`, `@1`, etc. resolve to the IDs of beads created at that position. Critical for mitosis — you don't know child IDs until they're created.\n586\t\n587\t**Atomicity**: All operations run inside a single SQLite write transaction (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`). If any operation fails (invalid ID, bad status transition), the transaction rolls back — no partial state is committed. Kill -9 during batch leaves the workspace in its original state.\n588\t\n589\t**Use cases**:\n590\t- NEEDLE mitosis: split 1 bead into N children (2N+1 operations → 1 batch)\n591\t- Bulk status changes: close all beads with a given label\n592\t- Migration: import beads from another tracker\n593\t- Workspace setup: create epics + child beads + dependencies in one shot\n594\t\n595\t**Output**: Returns JSON array of results, one per operation:\n596\t\n597\t```json\n598\t[\n599\t  {\"op\": 0, \"status\": \"ok\", \"id\": \"fg-b4d1\"},\n600\t  {\"op\": 1, \"status\": \"ok\", \"id\": \"fg-b4d2\"},\n601\t  {\"op\": 2, \"status\": \"ok\"},\n602\t  {\"op\": 3, \"status\": \"ok\"}\n603\t]\n604\t```\n605\t\n606\t### 4B.3 Annotations — Extensible Metadata (`src/model.rs`, `src/cli/annotate.rs`)\n607\t\n608\tArbitrary key-value pairs on any bead, stored in a **separate `bead_annotations` table** — never as a column on `issues`.\n609\t\n610\t**Why not a column on `issues`**: br's `issues_column_order_matches()` check in `schema.rs:630` fires on every `br` open and returns `false` the moment the actual column count differs from `EXPECTED_ISSUE_COLUMN_ORDER.len()`. This triggers `rebuild_issues_table()` which rewrites `issues` from br's canonical column list — destroying any bf-added columns. Adding an `annotations` column to `issues` would be silently wiped on every `br` invocation.\n611\t\n612\t**Schema** (br never touches this table):\n613\t\n614\t```sql\n615\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS bead_annotations (\n616\t    bead_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES issues(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n617\t    key     TEXT NOT NULL,\n618\t    value   TEXT NOT NULL,\n619\t    PRIMARY KEY (bead_id, key)\n620\t);\n621\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_bead_annotations_key_value\n622\t    ON bead_annotations (key, value);  -- for bf list --annotation key=value\n623\t```\n624\t\n625\t**Issue struct** (annotations loaded via JOIN, serialized into the JSONL field):\n626\t\n627\t```rust\n628\t#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = \"BTreeMap::is_empty\")]\n629\tpub annotations: BTreeMap<String, String>,\n630\t```\n631\t\n632\tJSONL export serializes annotations into the `Issue` JSON object. On import, annotations are upserted into `bead_annotations`. br ignores the `annotations` JSON field when reading JSONL (`skip_unknown_fields`), so JSONL round-trips are clean.\n633\t\n634\t**CLI**:\n635\t\n636\t```bash\n637\t# Set annotations\n638\tbf annotate fg-a3f8 needle_attempt=3 needle_session=abc123\n639\tbf annotate fg-a3f8 review_status=needs_review\n640\t\n641\t# Remove annotations\n642\tbf annotate fg-a3f8 --remove needle_error\n643\t\n644\t# Query by annotation\n645\tbf list --annotation needle_attempt=3\n646\tbf list --annotation review_status=needs_review\n647\t\n648\t# Show annotations (included in bf show output)\n649\tbf show fg-a3f8 --json | jq '.annotations'\n650\t```\n651\t\n652\t**br backward compatibility**: br never reads, writes, or rebuilds `bead_annotations`. When br opens a bf-migrated database: its `issues_column_order_matches()` check passes (no extra columns on `issues`), no rebuild fires, annotation data is untouched. When br creates a bead, `bead_annotations` has no row for it — that is correct, the bead just has no annotations yet.\n653\t\n654\t**Use cases**:\n655\t- NEEDLE: `needle_attempt`, `needle_session`, `needle_error`, `needle_worker_version`\n656\t- Custom tools: `ci_build_id`, `deployment_sha`, `jira_key`\n657\t- Fleet tracking: `claimed_at`, `heartbeat_at`, `estimated_minutes_remaining`\n658\t\n659\t### 4B.4 Operation History — `bf log` (`src/log.rs`)\n660\t\n661\tShow the history of mutations on a specific bead or workspace, sourced from the SQLite events table and git history.\n662\t\n663\t```bash\n664\tbf log fg-a3f8                          # full history of this bead\n665\tbf log --since \"2026-04-25\"             # all workspace changes since date\n666\tbf log --actor worker-3                 # changes by a specific worker\n667\tbf log --limit 20                       # last 20 mutations\n668\tbf log --status-changes                 # only status transitions\n669\t```\n670\t\n671\tOutput:\n672\t\n673\t```\n674\t2026-04-25 10:00  CREATED     by: human        \"Implement auth flow\"\n675\t2026-04-25 10:05  DEP_ADD     by: human        blocked by fg-b2c1\n676\t2026-04-25 14:30  CLAIMED     by: worker-3     status: open → in_progress\n677\t2026-04-25 15:45  COMMENT     by: worker-3     \"Found edge case in token refresh\"\n678\t2026-04-25 16:00  CLOSED      by: worker-3     \"Completed\"\n679\t```\n680\t\n681\t**Implementation**: History is sourced from two places:\n682\t\n683\t1. **SQLite events table** (primary): every mutation records an event row (`event_type`, `actor`, `old_value`, `new_value`). `bf log` queries this table directly — O(1) lookup by issue ID, no file scanning.\n684\t\n685\t2. **Git history** (fallback/deep history): `issues.jsonl` is committed to git on each sync. `git log -p .beads/issues.jsonl` shows every past state of the file. `bf log --git` uses this for history older than the events table retention window.\n686\t\n687\tSince JSONL has one entry per bead (not a mutation log), JSONL diffing is not used for history. The events table is the authoritative mutation record.\n688\t\n689\t**`bf log --diff`**: Show the field-level diff from the events table:\n690\t\n691\t```bash\n692\tbf log fg-a3f8 --diff\n693\t# 2026-04-25 14:30  CLAIMED\n694\t#   - status: \"open\" → \"in_progress\"\n695\t#   + assignee: null → \"worker-3\"\n696\t#   + annotations.needle_attempt: \"1\" → \"2\"\n697\t```\n698\t\n699\t### 4B.5 Critical Path Computation (`src/critical_path.rs`)\n700\t\n701\tComputes the longest dependency chain through the workspace graph and annotates each bead with its **float** — how many hops it can slip before delaying the epic. Beads with `float == 0` are on the critical path; every day they're blocked delays completion by a day.\n702\t\n703\t**Command**:\n704\t\n705\t```bash\n706\t$ bf critical-path fg-epic-auth\n707\t\n708\tCritical path for fg-epic-auth (14 open beads, 3 on critical path):\n709\t\n710\t  float=0  [fg-a3f8] Implement auth token refresh        in_progress (worker-3)\n711\t  float=0  [fg-b2c1] Fix concurrent session state bug    open\n712\t  float=0  [fg-c4d9] Integration tests: auth flow        open\n713\t  float=2  [fg-d5e3] Update API documentation            open\n714\t  float=2  [fg-e6f4] Refactor error response format      open\n715\t  float=5  [fg-f7g5] Add rate limiting headers           open\n716\t  ...\n717\t\n718\tLongest chain: fg-a3f8 → fg-b2c1 → fg-c4d9 → [epic closes]\n719\tMinimum remaining time: 3 bead-completions on critical path\n720\t```\n721\t\n722\t**Algorithm**: Two-pass walk on the dependency DAG using SQLite recursive CTEs.\n723\t\n724\tPass 1 (forward — compute earliest start `ES`): start from beads with no open predecessors (`ES=0`). For each bead, `ES = max(ES of all open predecessors) + 1`.\n725\t\n726\tPass 2 (backward — compute latest start `LS`): start from the epic. For each bead, `LS = min(LS of all successors) - 1`. `float = LS - ES`. Zero-float beads form the critical path.\n727\t\n728\t```sql\n729\tWITH RECURSIVE\n730\t  -- Forward pass: earliest start for each open bead\n731\t  forward(id, es) AS (\n732\t    SELECT i.id, 0\n733\t    FROM issues i\n734\t    WHERE i.status IN ('open', 'in_progress')\n735\t      AND NOT EXISTS (\n736\t        SELECT 1 FROM dependencies d\n737\t        JOIN issues pred ON pred.id = d.depends_on_id\n738\t        WHERE d.issue_id = i.id AND d.type = 'blocks'\n739\t          AND pred.status IN ('open', 'in_progress')\n740\t      )\n741\t    UNION ALL\n742\t    SELECT d.issue_id, MAX(f.es) + 1\n743\t    FROM dependencies d\n744\t    JOIN forward f ON f.id = d.depends_on_id\n745\t    WHERE d.type = 'blocks'\n746\t    GROUP BY d.issue_id\n747\t  )\n748\tSELECT id, es,\n749\t       (SELECT MAX(es) FROM forward) - es AS float\n750\tFROM forward\n751\tORDER BY float ASC, es ASC;\n752\t```\n753\t\n754\t**Schema addition**: `critical_path_cache` table — recomputed whenever a dependency is added/removed or a bead changes status. Invalidated by a trigger on `dependencies` and `issues.status`.\n755\t\n756\t```sql\n757\tCREATE TABLE critical_path_cache (\n758\t    bead_id     TEXT PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES issues(id),\n759\t    epic_id     TEXT REFERENCES issues(id),\n760\t    es          INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- earliest start (hops from root)\n761\t    ls          INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- latest start\n762\t    float       INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- ls - es; 0 = critical path\n763\t    updated_at  DATETIME NOT NULL\n764\t);\n765\t```\n766\t\n767\t**Integration with claim scoring**: The claim query joins `critical_path_cache` and adds a large bonus for zero-float beads:\n768\t\n769\t```sql\n770\t1000.0 / (COALESCE(cp.float, 999) + 1) AS critical_path_bonus\n771\t```\n772\t\n773\tA zero-float bead gets `1000 / 1 = 1000` bonus. A float-5 bead gets `1000 / 6 ≈ 167`. A bead not on any epic path gets `1000 / 1000 = 1`. This ensures critical-path beads always sort above non-critical beads regardless of their listed priority.\n774\t\n775\t**`bf dep add-blocker` side effect**: Every time a dependency is created or removed, the cache for the affected epic is invalidated and recomputed inside the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. Recompute cost is O(V + E) — for a 500-bead workspace with 300 deps, under 1ms.\n776\t\n777\t### 4B.6 Velocity-Aware Impact Scoring (`src/velocity.rs`)\n778\t\n779\tThe claim scorer currently orders by `downstream_impact / priority`. That's correct for maximizing throughput when all workers and task types take similar time. In practice, a `claude-opus-4-7` worker closes `bug` beads in 8 minutes while the same harness on `claude-haiku-4-5` takes 35 minutes for the same type — a 4× difference. The optimal claim for each worker is the task where `impact / expected_duration` is maximized for *that specific worker configuration*, not the global impact rank.\n780\t\n781\t**Worker metadata on claim**: Workers pass their composition to `bf claim`:\n782\t\n783\t```bash\n784\tbf claim --assignee worker-01 \\\n785\t         --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \\\n786\t         --harness needle \\\n787\t         --harness-version 0.5.2 \\\n788\t         --json\n789\t```\n790\t\n791\t**Schema**:\n792\t\n793\t```sql\n794\t-- One row per worker session (created on bf claim, updated on bf close)\n795\tCREATE TABLE worker_sessions (\n796\t    session_id      TEXT PRIMARY KEY,  -- UUID generated by worker\n797\t    worker_id       TEXT NOT NULL,\n798\t    model           TEXT,              -- 'claude-sonnet-4-6', 'claude-opus-4-7', etc.\n799\t    harness         TEXT,              -- 'needle', 'custom'\n800\t    harness_version TEXT,              -- '0.5.2'\n801\t    bead_id         TEXT REFERENCES issues(id),\n802\t    claimed_at      DATETIME,\n803\t    closed_at       DATETIME,\n804\t    duration_seconds INTEGER           -- populated on bf close\n805\t);\n806\t\n807\t-- Aggregated velocity statistics per (model, harness, issue_type)\n808\tCREATE TABLE velocity_stats (\n809\t    model           TEXT NOT NULL,\n810\t    harness         TEXT NOT NULL,\n811\t    issue_type      TEXT NOT NULL,\n812\t    sample_count    INTEGER DEFAULT 0,\n813\t    p50_seconds     INTEGER,\n814\t    p90_seconds     INTEGER,\n815\t    avg_seconds     REAL,\n816\t    last_updated    DATETIME,\n817\t    PRIMARY KEY (model, harness, issue_type)\n818\t);\n819\t```\n820\t\n821\t**Update on close**: When `bf close <id>` is called, if the closing worker passed `--session-id <uuid>`, the session row is updated with `closed_at` and `duration_seconds`. A trigger recomputes `velocity_stats` for that `(model, harness, issue_type)` tuple using a window over the last 50 sessions.\n822\t\n823\t**Claim scoring integration**: The claim query joins `velocity_stats` for the requesting worker and computes `score = impact / expected_seconds`:\n824\t\n825\t```sql\n826\tSELECT\n827\t    i.id,\n828\t    i.priority,\n829\t    i.issue_type,\n830\t    COALESCE(cp.float, 999)            AS critical_float,\n831\t    COALESCE(vs.p50_seconds, 1800)     AS expected_seconds,\n832\t    -- Combined score: more impact per unit time = higher priority\n833\t    (\n834\t      downstream_impact * 3.0\n835\t      + (4 - i.priority) * 2.0\n836\t      + 1000.0 / (COALESCE(cp.float, 999) + 1)\n837\t    ) / COALESCE(vs.p50_seconds, 1800) AS score\n838\tFROM issues i\n839\tLEFT JOIN critical_path_cache cp  ON cp.bead_id = i.id\n840\tLEFT JOIN velocity_stats vs       ON vs.issue_type = i.issue_type\n841\t                                 AND vs.model    = ?model\n842\t                                 AND vs.harness  = ?harness\n843\tWHERE i.status = 'open'\n844\t  AND i.id NOT IN (SELECT issue_id FROM blocked_issues_cache)\n845\tORDER BY score DESC\n846\tLIMIT 1\n847\t```\n848\t\n849\tWorkers with no velocity history use the 1800s (30-minute) default — conservative but correct. After ~10 completions per type, the estimate is meaningful.\n850\t\n851\t**`bf velocity` command**: Show the velocity table for all known worker configurations:\n852\t\n853\t```\n854\t$ bf velocity\n855\t\n856\tModel                  Harness   Type     Samples  p50    p90\n857\tclaude-opus-4-7        needle    task     142      8m     22m\n858\tclaude-opus-4-7        needle    bug      38       14m    51m\n859\tclaude-sonnet-4-6      needle    task     87       18m    45m\n860\tclaude-sonnet-4-6      needle    bug      24       31m    1h12m\n861\tclaude-haiku-4-5       needle    task     23       35m    2h10m\n862\t```\n863\t\n864\tThis table surfaces which model+harness combinations are most efficient for which task types — direct input for fleet composition decisions (which models to provision for which workspace).\n865\t\n866\t**Fallback**: If `--model` is not passed to `bf claim`, the scorer uses the average across all models for that issue type. Backward compatible — undecorated `bf claim` calls work exactly as before, just without per-model scoring.\n867\t\n868\t---\n869\t\n870\t## Phase 4C: br→bf Workspace Migration (`src/migrate.rs`)\n871\t\n872\tExisting repos using `br` migrate to `bf` without data loss. Three migration paths cover all scenarios.\n873\t\n874\t### Migration Path A — Drop-in Replace (zero downtime, zero data conversion)\n875\t\n876\tThe `br`→`bf` symlink is the migration for most repos:\n877\t\n878\t```bash\n879\tln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n880\t```\n881\t\n882\t`apply_migrations()` in `Storage::open` handles schema differences automatically on first open:\n883\t\n884\t```rust\n885\tfn apply_migrations(conn: &Connection) -> Result<()> {\n886\t    // All bf-only tables use CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS — no-op on existing DBs.\n887\t    // Annotations are stored in bead_annotations, NOT as a column on issues.\n888\t    // Reason: br's issues_column_order_matches() in schema.rs:630 fires on every\n889\t    // br open and triggers rebuild_issues_table() if the column count differs —\n890\t    // which silently destroys any extra column. bead_annotations is a separate\n891\t    // table br never reads, writes, or rebuilds.\n892\t    conn.execute_batch(\"\n893\t        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS bead_annotations (\n894\t            bead_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES issues(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n895\t            key     TEXT NOT NULL,\n896\t            value   TEXT NOT NULL,\n897\t            PRIMARY KEY (bead_id, key)\n898\t        );\n899\t        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_bead_annotations_key_value\n900\t            ON bead_annotations (key, value);\n901\t        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS worker_sessions ( ... );\n902\t        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS velocity_stats ( ... );\n903\t        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS critical_path_cache ( ... );\n904\t    \")?;\n905\t    Ok(())\n906\t}\n907\t```\n908\t\n909\tAll bf-only tables are created with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` — the call is a no-op when the database was already created by `bf`. No column migrations on `issues` are needed.\n910\t\n911\t**br backward compatibility after migration**: `br` opens a bf-migrated database and finds the `issues` table column count matches exactly — `issues_column_order_matches()` passes, `rebuild_issues_table()` never fires. br ignores `bead_annotations`, `worker_sessions`, `velocity_stats`, and `critical_path_cache` entirely. All `br` commands (list, show, update, doctor) continue working.\n912\t\n913\t### Migration Path B — `bf migrate` (explicit, with backup and verification)\n914\t\n915\tFor workspaces where you want an audit point and rollback safety:\n916\t\n917\t```bash\n918\tbf migrate /path/to/workspace [--dry-run]\n919\t```\n920\t\n921\tSteps:\n922\t1. **Pause fleet** (optional): write a `migration_lock` row to the workspace DB so `bf claim` refuses new claims during migration\n923\t2. **Backup**: `cp beads.db beads.db.br-backup-<timestamp>`\n924\t3. **Apply migrations**: call `apply_migrations()` explicitly\n925\t4. **Prime caches**: populate `critical_path_cache` for all epics in one pass\n926\t5. **Seed config**: add `bf`-specific keys with defaults to `config.yaml` if absent:\n927\t   ```yaml\n928\t   # bead-forge additions (br ignores these)\n929\t   claim_ttl_minutes: 30\n930\t   rotate_age_days: 30\n931\t   rotate_max_size_mb: 5\n932\t   ```\n933\t6. **Verify forward compat**: run `br doctor --db beads.db` — must exit 0\n934\t7. **Verify backward compat**: run `bf doctor --check` — must exit 0\n935\t8. **Release fleet**: remove `migration_lock`\n936\t\n937\tOutput:\n938\t```\n939\tMigrating /home/coding/FORGE/.beads\n940\t  ✓ Backed up beads.db → beads.db.br-backup-2026-04-30T09:15:00Z\n941\t  ✓ Applied schema migrations (created bead_annotations, worker_sessions, velocity_stats, critical_path_cache tables)\n942\t  ✓ Primed critical_path_cache for 3 epics (247 beads)\n943\t  ✓ Updated config.yaml with bf defaults\n944\t  ✓ br doctor: OK\n945\t  ✓ bf doctor: OK\n946\tMigration complete. br symlink: ln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n947\t```\n948\t\n949\t### Migration Path C — `bf migrate --from-jsonl` (events table lost)\n950\t\n951\tWhen `beads.db` is missing or corrupted beyond repair and only `issues.jsonl` is available (the `br doctor --repair` scenario). The standard reimport loses the `events` table — all claim/close history vanishes.\n952\t\n953\t`--from-jsonl` recovers history from git:\n954\t\n955\t```bash\n956\tbf migrate /path/to/workspace --from-jsonl [--seed-velocity]\n957\t```\n958\t\n959\t1. **Reimport**: stream `issues.jsonl` into fresh SQLite (standard import)\n960\t2. **Reconstruct events from git log**:\n961\t   ```bash\n962\t   git log --follow -p .beads/issues.jsonl\n963\t   ```\n964\t   For each commit that touched `issues.jsonl`:\n965\t   - Diff the JSONL before/after\n966\t   - New bead appearing → synthetic `created` event with commit timestamp + author\n967\t   - Bead status changing `open→in_progress` → synthetic `claimed` event\n968\t   - Bead status changing `in_progress→closed` → synthetic `closed` event + compute `duration_seconds`\n969\t   - Synthetic events get `metadata: {\"source\": \"git-reconstructed\", \"commit\": \"<sha>\"}`\n970\t3. **Seed velocity stats** (`--seed-velocity`): from reconstructed `closed` events with known actor, compute `(model=unknown, harness=unknown, issue_type)` averages. Actor name patterns (`worker-claude-sonnet-4-6-01`) can infer model if the naming convention was consistent.\n971\t\n972\tRecovery completeness depends on how often `bf sync` was run (each sync = one JSONL commit in git). With daily syncs: ~95% of events reconstructed. With hourly syncs: ~99%.\n973\t\n974\t### Migration Checklist for All Repos\n975\t\n976\t```bash\n977\t# Per-machine: install bf, symlink br\n978\tcurl -L https://github.com/jedarden/bead-forge/releases/latest/download/bf-linux-x86_64 \\\n979\t  -o ~/.local/bin/bf && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/bf\n980\tln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n981\t\n982\t# Per-workspace: explicit migration with backup\n983\tfor workspace in \\\n984\t  /home/coding/FORGE \\\n985\t  /home/coding/NEEDLE \\\n986\t  /home/coding/AgentScribe \\\n987\t  /home/coding/ARMOR \\\n988\t  /home/coding/SIGIL \\\n989\t  /home/coding/CLASP \\\n990\t  /home/coding/bead-forge; do\n991\t  bf migrate \"$workspace\"\n992\tdone\n993\t\n994\t# Update NEEDLE config to pass worker metadata\n995\t# In .config/needle/adapters/claude-sonnet.yaml, add to invoke_template:\n996\t#   bf claim --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --harness-version 0.5.2 ...\n997\t```\n998\t\n999\t### Schema Compatibility Matrix\n1000\t\n1001\t| Scenario | br behavior | Result |\n1002\t|----------|-------------|--------|\n1003\t| br opens bf-created DB | `issues` column count matches br's expected count exactly (no extra columns); br ignores `bead_annotations`, `worker_sessions`, `velocity_stats`, `critical_path_cache` | ✓ Full compat |\n1004\t| bf opens br-created DB | `apply_migrations()` creates `bead_annotations` + bf-only tables via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` | ✓ Transparent |\n1005\t| bf opens very old br DB (missing `issues` columns) | br's own `rebuild_issues_table()` handles this on next `br` open; bf schema also applies its own `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` for bf-only tables | ✓ Handled |\n1006\t| br and bf open same DB concurrently | `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is idempotent; WAL serializes writes | ✓ Safe |\n1007\t| bf opens bf-created DB | `apply_migrations()` is no-op — all tables already exist | ✓ Idempotent |\n1008\t\n1009\t---\n1010\t\n1011\t## Phase 5: NEEDLE Integration\n1012\t\n1013\tNEEDLE's `bead_store/mod.rs` chains several `br` CLI calls that have race windows between them. Each race is eliminated by the corresponding `bf` command.\n1014\t\n1015\t### 5.1 Race 1 — `claim()`: two-process verify (lines 518-558)\n1016\t\n1017\t**br (racy)**:\n1018\t```rust\n1019\t// Call 1: br update <id> --status in_progress --assignee <actor>\n1020\t// ← race window: another worker sees this bead as open and claims it ←\n1021\t// Call 2: br show <id>  (to verify .assignee == actor)\n1022\t```\n1023\tTwo processes, two SQLite opens. If two workers race, both issue `br update` and `br` does not enforce exclusivity — it just overwrites. The `br show` verify is too late: the wrong worker may have overwritten the `assignee` field between the two calls.\n1024\t\n1025\t**bf (atomic)**:\n1026\t```bash\n1027\tbead=$(bf claim --workspace \"$WORKSPACE\" --assignee \"$WORKER\" --json)\n1028\t```\n1029\t`bf claim` runs the full read-score-update sequence inside a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. No second call, no verify step needed.\n1030\t\n1031\t### 5.2 Race 2 — `ready()` → `claim()`: TOCTOU (lines 487-507)\n1032\t\n1033\t**br (racy)**:\n1034\t```rust\n1035\t// Call 1: br ready --json  → returns candidate list (a snapshot)\n1036\t// ← race window: another worker claims the top candidate ←\n1037\t// Call 2: claim(selected_id)  → may be claiming an already-claimed bead\n1038\t```\n1039\tThe winning bead is selected from a stale snapshot. By the time `claim()` issues `br update`, the bead may have been claimed by another worker.\n1040\t\n1041\t**bf (atomic)**:\n1042\t```bash\n1043\tbead=$(bf claim --workspace \"$WORKSPACE\" --assignee \"$WORKER\" --json)\n1044\t```\n1045\tScoring and claiming happen inside the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. The candidate list is read and the winner is updated atomically — no snapshot staleness.\n1046\t\n1047\t### 5.3 Race 3 — Mitosis: `create_bead` + `add_dependency` (lines 607-639)\n1048\t\n1049\t**br (non-atomic)**:\n1050\t```rust\n1051\t// Call 1: br create \"child title\"  → returns new bead ID\n1052\t// ← process crash here → orphaned bead with no dependency link ←\n1053\t// Call 2: br dep add <parent_id> <child_id>\n1054\t```\n1055\tIf NEEDLE dies between `br create` and `br dep add`, the child bead exists but is not linked to the parent. The parent is never closed; the orphaned child is never picked up.\n1056\t\n1057\t**bf (atomic)**:\n1058\t```bash\n1059\tbf batch --json '[\n1060\t  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"child 1\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n1061\t  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"child 2\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n1062\t  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"fg-a3f8.1\", \"child\": \"fg-a3f8\"},\n1063\t  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"fg-a3f8.2\", \"child\": \"fg-a3f8\"},\n1064\t  {\"op\": \"close\", \"id\": \"fg-a3f8\", \"reason\": \"Split into children\"}\n1065\t]'\n1066\t```\n1067\tAll five operations run inside one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. If NEEDLE dies mid-batch, the transaction rolls back automatically — no partial state.\n1068\t\n1069\t### 5.4 Inefficiency — `labels()` round-trip (lines 584-588)\n1070\t\n1071\t**br (wasteful)**:\n1072\t```rust\n1073\tasync fn labels(&self, id: &BeadId) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n1074\t    let bead = self.show(id).await?;  // full br show just to get .labels\n1075\t    Ok(bead.labels)\n1076\t}\n1077\t```\n1078\tOne full `br show` (with JSON deserialization of all 35 fields) to extract one field.\n1079\t\n1080\t**bf (direct)**:\n1081\t```bash\n1082\tbf labels <id> --json   # SELECT label FROM labels WHERE issue_id = ?\n1083\t```\n1084\tDirect `SELECT` on the `labels` table. Single column, single round-trip.\n1085\t\n1086\t### 5.5 Inefficiency — Multiple `br label add` calls\n1087\t\n1088\t**br (unbatched)**:\n1089\t```rust\n1090\tfor label in &labels {\n1091\t    br_label_add(id, label).await?;   // separate process per label\n1092\t}\n1093\t```\n1094\tN labels = N process invocations = N SQLite open/close cycles.\n1095\t\n1096\t**bf (batched)**:\n1097\t```bash\n1098\tbf label <id> add label1 label2 label3   # one INSERT per label, one transaction\n1099\t```\n1100\t\n1101\t### 5.6 Pluck Strand Update\n1102\t\n1103\tOne-line change in NEEDLE's `pluck.sh` or `bead_store/mod.rs`:\n1104\t\n1105\t```bash\n1106\t# Old (racy — two br calls with a race window between them):\n1107\t# br ready --json | pick_top | xargs br update --status in_progress --assignee \"$WORKER\"\n1108\t\n1109\t# New (atomic — single bf call, BEGIN IMMEDIATE claim):\n1110\tbead=$(bf claim --workspace \"$WORKSPACE\" --assignee \"$WORKER\" --json)\n1111\t```\n1112\t\n1113\t### 5.7 Close/Block Remains Same\n1114\t\n1115\t```bash\n1116\tbf close \"$bead_id\" --reason \"Completed: ...\"\n1117\tbf update \"$bead_id\" --status blocked\n1118\t```\n1119\t\n1120\tThese are single-operation writes with no race condition — no change needed.\n1121\t\n1122\t### 5.8 Symlink for Backward Compatibility\n1123\t\n1124\t```bash\n1125\tln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n1126\t```\n1127\t\n1128\tAll existing scripts using `br` commands work without modification. NEEDLE's non-atomic chains are replaced incrementally — `bf claim` and `bf batch` are the high-priority fixes; the rest can migrate when convenient.\n1129\t\n1130\t---\n1131\t\n1132\t## Phase 6: Build & Deploy\n1133\t\n1134\t### 6.1 Project Structure\n1135\t\n1136\t```\n1137\tbead-forge/\n1138\t├── Cargo.toml\n1139\t├── src/\n1140\t│   ├── main.rs              # CLI entry point\n1141\t│   ├── lib.rs               # Library root\n1142\t│   ├── model.rs             # Issue, Status, Priority, Annotations\n1143\t│   ├── jsonl.rs             # JSONL import (sync --import) + export (sync --flush)\n1144\t│   ├── config.rs            # Config + Metadata parsing\n1145\t│   ├── id.rs                # ID generation\n1146\t│   ├── claim.rs             # Atomic BEGIN IMMEDIATE claiming + impact scoring\n1147\t│   ├── rotate.rs            # JSONL log rotation\n1148\t│   ├── batch.rs             # Batch operations under single BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction\n1149\t│   ├── log.rs               # Operation history from JSONL event log\n1150\t│   ├── secrets.rs           # Secret pattern scanning\n1151\t│   ├── cli/\n1152\t│   │   ├── mod.rs           # clap app definition\n1153\t│   │   ├── create.rs\n1154\t│   │   ├── list.rs\n1155\t│   │   ├── show.rs\n1156\t│   │   ├── update.rs\n1157\t│   │   ├── close.rs\n1158\t│   │   ├── reopen.rs\n1159\t│   │   ├── delete.rs\n1160\t│   │   ├── ready.rs\n1161\t│   │   ├── claim.rs         # `bf claim` subcommand\n1162\t│   │   ├── annotate.rs      # `bf annotate` subcommand\n1163\t│   │   ├── batch.rs         # `bf batch` subcommand\n1164\t│   │   ├── log.rs           # `bf log` subcommand\n1165\t│   │   ├── rotate.rs        # `bf rotate` subcommand\n1166\t│   │   ├── dep.rs\n1167\t│   │   ├── label.rs\n1168\t│   │   ├── comment.rs\n1169\t│   │   ├── sync.rs\n1170\t│   │   ├── doctor.rs\n1171\t│   │   ├── init.rs\n1172\t│   │   ├── search.rs\n1173\t│   │   └── stats.rs\n1174\t│   ├── storage/\n1175\t│   │   ├── mod.rs\n1176\t│   │   ├── schema.rs        # SQLite DDL (identical to br)\n1177\t│   │   └── sqlite.rs        # rusqlite storage backend\n1178\t│   └── format/\n1179\t│       ├── mod.rs\n1180\t│       ├── text.rs\n1181\t│       ├── json.rs\n1182\t│       └── toon.rs\n1183\t├── tests/\n1184\t│   ├── common/\n1185\t│   │   └── mod.rs           # Shared test harness — TempWorkspace, fixture loading\n1186\t│   ├── jsonl_compat.rs      # Round-trip tests with br JSONL files (read-only fixtures)\n1187\t│   ├── schema_compat.rs     # Schema match + forward-compat: bf writes, br reads\n1188\t│   ├── claim_race.rs        # Concurrent BEGIN IMMEDIATE claim tests (isolated tempdir)\n1189\t│   ├── batch_atomic.rs      # Batch rollback on failure\n1190\t│   ├── br_isolation.rs      # Verify bf never touches live br workspaces\n1191\t│   └── secret_scanning.rs   # Secret pattern detection\n1192\t└── build.rs\n1193\t```\n1194\t\n1195\t### 6.2 Dependencies\n1196\t\n1197\t```toml\n1198\t[dependencies]\n1199\tclap = { version = \"4\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n1200\tserde = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n1201\tserde_json = \"1\"\n1202\tserde_yaml = \"0.9\"\n1203\tchrono = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n1204\trusqlite = { version = \"0.31\", features = [\"bundled\"] }\n1205\tsha2 = \"0.10\"\n1206\trand = \"0.8\"\n1207\tregex = \"1\"                 # Secret pattern scanning\n1208\ttracing = \"0.1\"\n1209\ttracing-subscriber = \"0.3\"\n1210\tanyhow = \"1\"\n1211\tthiserror = \"1\"\n1212\t\n1213\t[dev-dependencies]\n1214\ttempfile = \"3\"              # Ephemeral test workspaces — never touch live br workspaces\n1215\t```\n1216\t\n1217\t### 6.3 CI via Argo Workflows\n1218\t\n1219\tAdd a `bead-forge-build` WorkflowTemplate to `jedarden/declarative-config`. Builds the Rust binary in a container, pushes to `ronaldraygun/bead-forge`.\n1220\t\n1221\t### 6.4 Deployment\n1222\t\n1223\tDeploy as a standalone binary to the Hetzner server at `~/.local/bin/bf`. Symlink `br` -> `bf` for backward compatibility. No daemon, no systemd unit — each `bf` invocation is self-contained.\n1224\t\n1225\t---\n1226\t\n1227\t## Test Strategy & Isolation\n1228\t\n1229\tbf must be testable without touching, modifying, or interfering with any live `br` workspace. Three isolation concerns drive the test strategy:\n1230\t\n1231\t### Rule 1: Never Share a Live Database\n1232\t\n1233\tbf tests **never** auto-discover or open a `.beads/beads.db` that `br` is actively using. SQLite WAL mode serializes writers, so concurrent opens are safe from a corruption standpoint — but test mutations (creates, claims, closes) would contaminate real workspaces with spurious beads.\n1234\t\n1235\tAll tests target exactly one of:\n1236\t- **`:memory:`** — rusqlite in-memory database (unit tests, zero disk I/O)\n1237\t- **`tempfile::tempdir()`** — ephemeral directory deleted on drop (integration tests)\n1238\t- **Read-only fixture copy** — `issues.jsonl` copied out of a real workspace into a tempdir, never written back (compat tests)\n1239\t\n1240\t### Rule 2: Never Point `br` at a bf Test Database\n1241\t\n1242\tThe schema compat test must verify `br` can read a `bf`-created database — but it cannot use br's own workspace for this. The test creates a fresh tempdir, runs `bf init` + `bf create` in it, then invokes `br --db /path/to/temp/beads.db` against that file. The real `br` workspace is untouched.\n1243\t\n1244\t### Rule 3: JSONL Fixture = Read-Only Copy\n1245\t\n1246\tThe JSONL round-trip test uses a snapshot of a real workspace's `issues.jsonl` as a fixture, not a live file. The fixture is checked into `tests/fixtures/` (or copied at test time with `--allow-stale`). `bf sync --import` loads it into a tempdir database; `bf list --format json` is compared against the expected output derived from `br list --format json` run against the same snapshot.\n1247\t\n1248\t```\n1249\ttests/fixtures/\n1250\t├── forge-snapshot.jsonl    # snapshot of FORGE workspace (committed, static)\n1251\t└── needle-snapshot.jsonl   # snapshot of NEEDLE workspace\n1252\t```\n1253\t\n1254\tThese fixtures are updated manually when the schema changes: `cp /home/coding/FORGE/.beads/issues.jsonl tests/fixtures/forge-snapshot.jsonl`.\n1255\t\n1256\t### Test Harness (`tests/common/mod.rs`)\n1257\t\n1258\t```rust\n1259\tpub struct TempWorkspace {\n1260\t    dir: tempfile::TempDir,   // deleted on drop — no cleanup needed\n1261\t    pub db_path: PathBuf,\n1262\t    pub storage: Storage,\n1263\t}\n1264\t\n1265\timpl TempWorkspace {\n1266\t    pub fn new() -> Result<Self> {\n1267\t        let dir = tempfile::tempdir()?;\n1268\t        let db_path = dir.path().join(\".beads/beads.db\");\n1269\t        fs::create_dir(dir.path().join(\".beads\"))?;\n1270\t        let storage = Storage::open(&db_path)?;\n1271\t        Ok(Self { dir, db_path, storage })\n1272\t    }\n1273\t\n1274\t    pub fn from_fixture(jsonl: &Path) -> Result<Self> {\n1275\t        let ws = Self::new()?;\n1276\t        import_jsonl(jsonl, &ws.storage)?;\n1277\t        Ok(ws)\n1278\t    }\n1279\t\n1280\t    // Run br against this workspace's db (for compat tests)\n1281\t    pub fn br(&self, args: &[&str]) -> std::process::Output {\n1282\t        Command::new(\"br\")\n1283\t            .arg(\"--db\").arg(&self.db_path)\n1284\t            .args(args)\n1285\t            .output()\n1286\t            .expect(\"br not in PATH\")\n1287\t    }\n1288\t}\n1289\t```\n1290\t\n1291\tUnit tests in `#[cfg(test)]` modules use `Storage::open(\":memory:\")` directly — no `TempWorkspace` needed.\n1292\t\n1293\t### Schema Forward-Compatibility\n1294\t\n1295\tbf adds tables that br doesn't know about. No columns are added to the shared `issues` table.\n1296\t\n1297\t| Addition | br behavior |\n1298\t|----------|-------------|\n1299\t| `bead_annotations` table | br ignores unknown tables entirely; `issues` column count is unchanged so `issues_column_order_matches()` passes and `rebuild_issues_table()` never fires |\n1300\t| `worker_sessions` table | Same — ignored |\n1301\t| `velocity_stats` table | Same — ignored |\n1302\t| `critical_path_cache` table | Same — ignored |\n1303\t\n1304\t**Why no column on `issues`**: br's `issues_column_order_matches()` in `schema.rs:630` checks `actual_columns.len() != EXPECTED_ISSUE_COLUMN_ORDER.len()`. Any extra column causes this to return `false`, which triggers `rebuild_issues_table()` — a full DROP + recreate using only br's canonical column list. An `annotations` column on `issues` would be silently destroyed on every `br` open. Storing annotations in `bead_annotations` sidesteps this entirely.\n1305\t\n1306\tMigration on `Storage::open` (fully idempotent — all `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`):\n1307\t\n1308\t```rust\n1309\tfn apply_migrations(conn: &Connection) -> Result<()> {\n1310\t    conn.execute_batch(\"\n1311\t        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS bead_annotations (\n1312\t            bead_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES issues(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n1313\t            key     TEXT NOT NULL,\n1314\t            value   TEXT NOT NULL,\n1315\t            PRIMARY KEY (bead_id, key)\n1316\t        );\n1317\t        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_bead_annotations_key_value\n1318\t            ON bead_annotations (key, value);\n1319\t        -- other bf-only tables follow\n1320\t    \")?;\n1321\t    Ok(())\n1322\t}\n1323\t```\n1324\t\n1325\tNo `ALTER TABLE` on `issues`, ever. The migration is a pure table-creation pass.\n1326\t\n1327\t---\n1328\t\n1329\t## Compatibility Verification\n1330\t\n1331\tAll tests run against **isolated temp workspaces** — no live br workspace is touched.\n1332\t\n1333\t### JSONL Round-Trip Test\n1334\t\n1335\t```bash\n1336\t# 1. Copy real workspace JSONL as a read-only fixture (never modify the original)\n1337\tFIXTURE=$(mktemp -d)\n1338\tcp /home/coding/FORGE/.beads/issues.jsonl \"$FIXTURE/issues.jsonl\"\n1339\t\n1340\t# 2. bf imports the fixture into a fresh tempdir\n1341\tBFDIR=$(mktemp -d)\n1342\tbf init --prefix fg --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"\n1343\tbf sync --import-only --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\" --jsonl \"$FIXTURE/issues.jsonl\"\n1344\t\n1345\t# 3. bf exports its view of the data\n1346\tbf list --format json --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\" | jq -S '.' > /tmp/bf-output.jsonl\n1347\t\n1348\t# 4. br reads the original JSONL directly (read-only, not our tempdir)\n1349\tbr list --format json --db \"$FIXTURE/issues.jsonl\" --no-db | jq -S '.' > /tmp/br-output.jsonl\n1350\t\n1351\t# 5. Compare (order-independent by ID)\n1352\tdiff /tmp/bf-output.jsonl /tmp/br-output.jsonl\n1353\t# Must be empty — bf and br produce identical output from the same JSONL\n1354\t\n1355\t# Cleanup\n1356\trm -rf \"$FIXTURE\" \"$BFDIR\"\n1357\t```\n1358\t\n1359\t### Schema Forward-Compatibility Test\n1360\t\n1361\t```bash\n1362\t# 1. bf creates a workspace in a fresh tempdir (bf schema, including bf-only tables)\n1363\tBFDIR=$(mktemp -d)\n1364\tbf init --prefix fg --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"\n1365\tbf create --title \"compat test\" --type task --priority 1 --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"\n1366\tbf create --title \"another bead\" --type bug --priority 0 --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"\n1367\tbf annotate fg-xxx needle_attempt=1 --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"\n1368\t# Annotation is stored in bead_annotations table, NOT in issues column\n1369\t\n1370\t# 2. br opens the same file (not its own workspace — pointed explicitly at the temp db)\n1371\tbr list --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"         # must list 2 beads without error\n1372\tbr doctor --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"       # must pass integrity check\n1373\t# Key assertion: br's issues_column_order_matches() sees exact column count → passes\n1374\t# rebuild_issues_table() is NOT triggered → bead_annotations table untouched\n1375\tbr update fg-xxx --status closed --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\"  # must succeed\n1376\t\n1377\t# 3. bf re-reads the br-mutated database (bead_annotations must survive br's UPDATE)\n1378\tbf show fg-xxx --db \"$BFDIR/beads.db\" --json | jq '.annotations'\n1379\t# Must still contain {\"needle_attempt\": \"1\"} — annotation row in bead_annotations\n1380\t# is untouched by br's UPDATE to the issues table\n1381\t\n1382\t# Cleanup\n1383\trm -rf \"$BFDIR\"\n1384\t```\n1385\t\n1386\t### Reverse Compatibility Test\n1387\t\n1388\t```bash\n1389\t# 1. br creates a workspace (br schema — no bead_annotations table, no velocity_stats table)\n1390\tBRDIR=$(mktemp -d)\n1391\tbr init --prefix fg --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\"\n1392\tbr create \"br-created bead\" --type task --priority 2 --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\"\n1393\t\n1394\t# 2. bf opens the br-created database\n1395\t# Storage::open runs apply_migrations() which creates bead_annotations + other bf-only\n1396\t# tables via CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS. No ALTER TABLE on issues is needed.\n1397\tbf list --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\"         # must list the br-created bead without error\n1398\tbf annotate fg-yyy test_key=hello --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\"  # inserts into bead_annotations\n1399\tbf show fg-yyy --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\" --json | jq '.annotations'\n1400\t# Must contain {\"test_key\": \"hello\"} — fetched via JOIN on bead_annotations\n1401\t\n1402\t# 3. br can still read the migrated database (new tables are ignored by br)\n1403\t# Critical: issues table column count is still exactly what br expects\n1404\tbr list --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\"         # must still work\n1405\tbr doctor --db \"$BRDIR/beads.db\"       # must still pass — no issues column changes\n1406\t\n1407\t# Cleanup\n1408\trm -rf \"$BRDIR\"\n1409\t```\n1410\t\n1411\t### Claim Race Test\n1412\t\n1413\t```bash\n1414\t# Setup: create a test workspace with 10 beads of varying priority\n1415\tcd /tmp && rm -rf bf-test && mkdir bf-test && cd bf-test && bf init --prefix bf\n1416\tfor i in $(seq 1 10); do\n1417\t  bf create --title \"test-$i\" --priority $((i % 5))\n1418\tdone\n1419\t\n1420\t# === Test 1: Basic thundering herd (20 workers, 10 beads) ===\n1421\t# 20 workers claim simultaneously. Exactly 10 get a bead, 10 get NONE.\n1422\tfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n1423\t  bf claim --any --assignee \"worker-$i\" --json > /tmp/claim-$i.out 2>&1 &\n1424\tdone\n1425\twait\n1426\t\n1427\t# Verify: exactly 10 claims succeeded, 10 returned NONE\n1428\tgrep -cl '\"id\"' /tmp/claim-*.out | wc -l  # must be 10\n1429\tgrep -cl 'NONE' /tmp/claim-*.out | wc -l  # must be 10\n1430\t\n1431\t# Verify: no bead claimed by more than one worker\n1432\tcat /tmp/claim-*.out | jq -r '.id' | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn\n1433\t# Every ID must appear exactly once\n1434\t\n1435\t# === Test 2: Priority ordering ===\n1436\t# The 10 workers that succeeded should have beads in priority order\n1437\t# (priority 0 beads claimed before priority 4)\n1438\t# Verify by correlating claim output order with priority values\n1439\t\n1440\t# === Test 3: Sequential claims (depletion) ===\n1441\t# Create 20 more beads, claim one at a time, verify queue drains\n1442\tfor i in $(seq 11 30); do\n1443\t  bf create --title \"test-$i\" --priority $((i % 5))\n1444\tdone\n1445\tfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n1446\t  result=$(bf claim --any --assignee \"seq-worker-$i\" --json)\n1447\t  [ -n \"$result\" ] && echo \"$result\" | jq -r '.id'\n1448\tdone\n1449\tbf list --status open --format json\n1450\t# Must return empty — all beads claimed\n1451\t\n1452\t# === Test 4: Stale claim reclamation ===\n1453\t# Claim a bead, manually age its updated_at past claim_ttl\n1454\t# Then claim again — the stale bead should be reclaimed and re-claimable\n1455\tbf claim --any --assignee \"dead-worker\" --json\n1456\t# (test harness: set claim_ttl to 5s for this test, or mock updated_at)\n1457\tbf doctor --reclaim-stale --ttl 0s\n1458\tbf list --status open --format json\n1459\t# The dead-worker's bead must be back in open status\n1460\t\n1461\t# === Test 5: Stale reclamation as side effect ===\n1462\t# Dead worker's bead is reclaimed when another worker claims\n1463\tbf claim --any --assignee \"scavenger\" --json\n1464\t# scavenger gets a bead; dead-worker's bead is also reclaimed to open\n1465\t\n1466\t# === Test 6: Multi-workspace claiming ===\n1467\t# Create two workspaces, claim --any gets highest priority across both\n1468\tmkdir /tmp/ws-a && cd /tmp/ws-a && bf init --prefix wa\n1469\tmkdir /tmp/ws-b && cd /tmp/ws-b && bf init --prefix wb\n1470\tbf create --title \"ws-a high\" --priority 0   # in ws-b\n1471\tbf create --title \"ws-a low\"  --priority 4   # in ws-b\n1472\tcd /tmp/ws-a && bf create --title \"ws-a mid\" --priority 2\n1473\tbf claim --any --assignee \"multi-worker\" --json --workspace /tmp/ws-a --workspace /tmp/ws-b\n1474\t# Should get ws-b's \"ws-a high\" (priority 0)\n1475\t```\n1476\t\n1477\t---\n1478\t\n1479\t## Implementation Order\n1480\t\n1481\t| Phase | Description | Estimated Effort |\n1482\t|-------|-------------|-----------------|\n1483\t| 1 | Core library (model, JSONL read/write, config, ID gen) | Foundation |\n1484\t| 2 | SQLite cache layer (schema, storage, sync, doctor) | Medium |\n1485\t| 3 | CLI commands (all br-compatible commands) | Medium |\n1486\t| 4 | Concurrent claiming (BEGIN IMMEDIATE, impact scoring, stale reclamation, multi-workspace) | Medium |\n1487\t| 4B | Extended features (log rotation, batch ops, annotations, operation history, secret scanning, critical path, velocity scoring) | Medium |\n1488\t| 5 | NEEDLE integration (pluck.sh update, backward compat) | Small |\n1489\t| 6 | Build & deploy (CI, binary) | Small |\n1490\t\n1491\tPhases 1-3 produce a fully functional standalone `bf` that replaces `br`. Phase 4 adds atomic claiming for fleet operations. Phase 4B adds extended features that make bf more powerful than br. Phases 5-6 integrate and deploy.\n1492\t\n1493\t---\n1494\t\n1495\t## Key Design Decisions\n1496\t\n1497\t1. **SQLite-primary, JSONL as git artifact**: SQLite is the live database — all reads and writes go through it. JSONL is exported on `bf sync` for git commit, providing backup, cross-machine sharing, and recovery. This matches how bd and br work, but with rusqlite (no corruption) and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for atomic claim (no race condition).\n1498\t\n1499\t2. **rusqlite, not FrankenSQLite**: We already proved in the beads_rust fork that FrankenSQLite has corruption issues. Start clean with rusqlite.\n1500\t\n1501\t3. **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for atomic claim**: The read-score-update sequence runs in a single SQLite write transaction. No flock, no streaming rewrite, no temp files. SQLite's own locking serializes concurrent claims correctly.\n1502\t\n1503\t4. **No automatic git commits**: Match br's explicit, non-invasive philosophy. Users run `bf sync` when they want to commit JSONL changes.\n1504\t\n1505\t5. **Cattle, not pets**: Workers are disposable fleet units with no identity, no memory, and no conversation. Claims are atomic SQL dequeues from a priority queue, not negotiations.\n1506\t\n1507\t6. **Stale reclamation as a side effect of claiming**: The first statement in every `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` claim transaction resets expired `in_progress` beads. No separate heartbeat system, no background process.\n1508\t\n1509\t7. **Server as future optimization, not v1**: A persistent in-memory server would reduce per-claim latency from ~2ms to ~0.1ms by eliminating SQLite file I/O. Premature for current scale — can be added transparently later.\n1510\t\n1511\t8. **Rotation keeps JSONL artifact lean**: Closed beads older than `rotate_age_days` are exported to archive files (`issues.jsonl.1`, etc.) rather than the active `issues.jsonl`. Keeps the committed artifact small and git diffs readable.\n1512\t\n1513\t9. **Annotations as extension mechanism**: Arbitrary `BTreeMap<String, String>` on beads lets external tools (NEEDLE, custom scripts) store structured metadata without schema changes. Stored in a separate `bead_annotations` table — never as a column on `issues` — because br's `issues_column_order_matches()` triggers `rebuild_issues_table()` on any column count mismatch, silently destroying any extra column on every br open. br never reads or writes `bead_annotations`, so annotation data survives any br operation unchanged.\n1514\t\n1515\t10. **Events table for history**: Every mutation records an event row. `bf log` queries the events table — O(1) by issue ID. Long-term history via git log on `issues.jsonl`.\n1516\t\n1517\t11. **Secret scanning before write**: Built-in regex-based secret detection prevents credentials from entering SQLite or the JSONL export. Configurable patterns, git pre-commit hook integration.\n1518\t\n1519\t12. **`.beads/` is committed to git**: `issues.jsonl` + config are version-controlled. `beads.db` is gitignored (binary, rebuilt from JSONL on `bf sync --import`). Secret scanning protects the exported JSONL.\n1520\t\n1521\t13. **Critical path drives claim priority, not just listed priority**: Listed priority is a human signal, but it decays as the project evolves and humans stop curating it. Critical path float is computed from the live dependency graph and is always current. A priority-3 bead on the critical path outranks a priority-0 bead with float=5 — the fleet naturally works on what matters most without human recuration.\n1522\t\n1523\t14. **Worker composition is first-class metadata**: `--model`, `--harness`, `--harness-version` on `bf claim` record what kind of agent did the work.
